The worker is idempotent,\n * resumable, and network-aware.  It is the single entry-point for any background sync.\n *\n * The worker is executed by the androidx.work.WorkManager according to a set of sensible\n * constraints and retry policies.  All exceptions are carefully mapped to\n * {@link Result} values so callers know whether to retry, fail, or mark success.\n */\npublic class SyncWorker extends Worker {\n\n    /* **********************  CONSTANTS  ************************* */\n\n    /**\n     * Unique name for the periodic work so that the WorkManager replaces duplicates.\n     */\n    public static final String WORK_NAME_PERIODIC = \"com.wellsphere.connect.SYNC_PERIODIC\";\n\n    /**\n     * Tag attached to all sync requests for easier diagnostics and cancellation.\n     */\n    public static final String WORK_TAG_SYNC = \"TAG_SYNC_WORK\";\n\n    /**\n     * Maximum back-off time in case of repeated transient errors.\n     */\n    private static final int MAX_BACKOFF_MINUTES = 30;\n\n    /* **********************  MEMBERS  *************************** */\n\n    private final SyncEngine                syncEngine;\n    private final OfflineRecordRepository   offlineRepository;\n    private final SyncMetadataRepository    metadataRepository;\n    private final SessionManager            sessionManager;\n    private final CrashReporter             crashReporter;\n\n    /* **********************  CONSTRUCTOR  *********************** */\n\n    public SyncWorker(@NonNull Context appContext, @NonNull WorkerParameters params) {\n        super(appContext, params);\n\n        /*\n         * In production the dependencies would be provided through a DI\n         * framework (e.g. Hilt).  For brevity, we fall back to service\n         * locators / singleton instances.\n         */\n        this.syncEngine          = SyncEngine.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.offlineRepository   = OfflineRecordRepository.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.metadataRepository  = SyncMetadataRepository.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.sessionManager      = SessionManager.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.crashReporter       = CrashReporter.getInstance();\n    }\n\n    /* **********************  WORK EXECUTION  ******************** */\n\n    @NonNull\n    @Override\n    public Result doWork() {\n        // 1. Abort early if user is not authenticated.\n        if (!sessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()) {\n            return Result.failure();\n        }\n\n        // 2. Verify that we still have connectivity. WorkManager handles\n        //    most of this but we double-check to fail fast.\n        if (!NetworkUtil.isConnected(getApplicationContext())) {\n            return Result.retry();\n        }\n\n        try {\n            // 3. Perform the actual sync.\n            performSync();\n\n            // 4. Success is reported only if both push/pull completed\n            //    without any exception.\n            return Result.success();\n\n        } catch (MergeConflictException conflict) {\n            /*\n             * Merge conflicts should NEVER crash the app.  They are business\n             * errors that need user attention.  We mark the work as successful\n             * so that WorkManager does not retry automatically, but we\n             * generate a notification so the user can resolve the problem.\n             */\n            conflict.printStackTrace();\n            crashReporter.logNonFatal(conflict);\n            notifyMergeConflict();\n            return Result.success();\n\n        } catch (SyncException | IllegalStateException recoverable) {\n            /*\n             * Expected transient failures: network blips, 5xx, JSON parse,\n             * encryption key rotations, etc.  Ask WorkManager to retry with\n             * exponential back-off.\n             */\n            recoverable.printStackTrace();\n            crashReporter.logNonFatal(recoverable);\n            return Result.retry();\n\n        } catch (Exception nasty) {\n            /*\n             * Unknown fatal errors: out-of-memory, database corruption, etc.\n             * We let WorkManager know that this work FAILED so it will stop\n             * retrying.  Crashlytics et al. will receive the stacktrace via\n             * CrashReporter.\n             */\n            nasty.printStackTrace();\n            crashReporter.log(nasty);\n            return Result.failure();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Executes the three-phase sync transaction:\n     *   1) Push local pending changes\n     *   2) Pull remote changes\n     *   3) Update sync metadata\n     *\n     * The method is isolated mainly for testability.\n     */\n    private void performSync() throws SyncException, MergeConflictException {\n        /* === phase 1: PUSH === */\n        syncEngine.pushPending(offlineRepository.fetchPendingRecords());\n\n        /* === phase 2: PULL === */\n        syncEngine.pullUpdates(metadataRepository.getLastSyncToken());\n\n        /* === phase 3: COMMIT === */\n        metadataRepository.setLastSyncTimestamp(System.currentTimeMillis());\n    }\n\n    /* **********************  PUBLIC API  ************************ */\n\n    /**\n     * Immediately enqueue a one-off sync.  This is used after the user\n     * creates new data while online, or when the user manually pulls-to-refresh.\n     */\n    public static void enqueueOneOffSync(@NonNull Context context) {\n        OneTimeWorkRequest request = new OneTimeWorkRequest.Builder(SyncWorker.class)\n                .addTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC)\n                .setBackoffCriteria(\n                        BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL,\n                        WorkManager.MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS,\n                        TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)\n                .setConstraints(defaultConstraints())\n                .build();\n\n        WorkManager\n                .getInstance(context)\n                .enqueue(request);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Schedule (or replace) the periodic background sync.  Called once on\n     * login and whenever the user toggles sync preferences.\n     */\n    public static void schedulePeriodicSync(@NonNull Context context, long repeatIntervalMinutes) {\n        PeriodicWorkRequest periodicWork = new PeriodicWorkRequest.Builder(\n                        SyncWorker.class,\n                        repeatIntervalMinutes,\n                        TimeUnit.MINUTES)\n                .addTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC)\n                .setBackoffCriteria(\n                        BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL,\n                        WorkManager.MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS,\n                        TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)\n                .setConstraints(defaultConstraints())\n                .build();\n\n        WorkManager\n                .getInstance(context)\n                .enqueueUniquePeriodicWork(\n                        WORK_NAME_PERIODIC,\n                        ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.UPDATE,\n                        periodicWork);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Cancels all scheduled or running sync jobs.  This is typically invoked\n     * on logout or when the user revokes permissions.\n     */\n    public static void cancelAllSync(@NonNull Context context) {\n        WorkManager.getInstance(context).cancelAllWorkByTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC);\n    }\n\n    /* **********************  HELPERS  *************************** */\n\n    private static Constraints defaultConstraints() {\n        return new Constraints.Builder()\n                .setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)\n                .setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)       // don't kill battery\n                .setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)       // need room for media tmp files\n                .build();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Sends a high-priority notification prompting the user to resolve merge\n     * conflicts.  Implementation omitted for brevity.\n     */\n    private void notifyMergeConflict() {\n        // TODO: Implement notification with deep-link to in-app conflict UI\n    }\n}\n\n/* ********************************************************************\n *  Below are minimal placeholder classes and utilities so that this\n *  single file compiles independently.  In production they live in\n *  proper modules/packages and are fully featured.\n * ******************************************************************** */\n\n/* -------- core/util/NetworkUtil ------------------------------------------------ */\nclass NetworkUtil {\n    static boolean isConnected(Context ctx) {\n        ConnectivityManager cm = (ConnectivityManager) ctx.getSystemService(Context.CONNECTIVITY_SERVICE);\n        if (cm == null) return false;\n        NetworkInfo info = cm.getActiveNetworkInfo();\n        return info != null && info.isConnected();\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------- core/analytics/CrashReporter ---------------------------------------- */\nclass CrashReporter {\n    private static volatile CrashReporter sInstance;\n\n    static CrashReporter getInstance() {\n        if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new CrashReporter();\n        return sInstance;\n    }\n\n    void log(Throwable t) { /* send to crashlytics */ }\n\n    void logNonFatal(Throwable t) { /* send non-fatal */ }\n}\n\n/* -------- core/auth/SessionManager -------------------------------------------- */\nclass SessionManager {\n    private static volatile SessionManager sInstance;\n    private final EncryptedPrefs prefs = EncryptedPrefs.getInstance();\n\n    static SessionManager getInstance(Context ctx) {\n        if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new SessionManager();\n        return sInstance;\n    }\n    boolean isUserAuthenticated() { return prefs.getBoolean(\"auth\", false); }\n}\n\n/* -------- core/storage/EncryptedPrefs ----------------------------------------- */\nclass EncryptedPrefs {\n    private static volatile EncryptedPrefs sInstance;\n    static EncryptedPrefs getInstance() { if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new EncryptedPrefs(); return sInstance; }\n    boolean getBoolean(String k, boolean d) { return d; }\n}\n\n/* -------- service/sync/repository/OfflineRecordRepository ---------------------- */\nclass OfflineRecordRepository {\n    static OfflineRecordRepository getInstance(Context ctx) { return new OfflineRecordRepository(); }\n    java.util.List<String> fetchPendingRecords() { return java.util.Collections.emptyList(); }\n}\n\n/* -------- service/sync/repository/SyncMetadataRepository ----------------------- */\nclass SyncMetadataRepository {\n    private long lastToken = 0;\n    static SyncMetadataRepository getInstance(Context ctx) { return new SyncMetadataRepository(); }\n    long getLastSyncToken() { return lastToken; }\n    void setLastSyncTimestamp(long ts) { lastToken = ts; }\n}\n\n/* -------- service/sync/engine/SyncEngine -------------------------------------- */\nclass SyncEngine {\n    private static volatile SyncEngine sInstance;\n    static SyncEngine getInstance(Context ctx) { if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new SyncEngine(); return sInstance; }\n\n    void pushPending(java.util.List<String> records) throws SyncException { /* push */ }\n\n    void pullUpdates(long lastToken) throws SyncException, MergeConflictException { /* pull */ }\n}\n\n/* -------- service/sync/engine custom exceptions ------------------------------- */\nclass SyncException extends Exception {\n    SyncException() { super(); }\n    SyncException(String m) { super(m); }\n}\nclass MergeConflictException extends Exception {\n    MergeConflictException() { super(); }\n    MergeConflictException(String m) { super(m); }\n}\n```","numLines":317,"startLine":1,"totalLines":317}}}

extends SyncableEntity>> customStrategyCache\n            = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n\n    /**\n     * Private constructor to enforce singleton usage.\n     */\n    private SyncConflictResolver() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Returns the singleton instance.\n     *\n     * @return An initialized, shared {@link SyncConflictResolver}.\n     */\n    public static SyncConflictResolver getInstance() {\n        if (instance == null) {\n            synchronized (SyncConflictResolver.class) {\n                if (instance == null) {\n                    instance = new SyncConflictResolver();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        return instance;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves a pair of entities that represent the same logical record but\n     * originate from different sources (local vs. remote).\n     *\n     * @param local   The entity stored on the device (may be {@code null} for pure inserts).\n     * @param remote  The entity retrieved from the server (may be {@code null} for local-only data).\n     * @param <T>     Concrete entity type implementing {@link SyncableEntity}.\n     *\n     * @return The authoritative version of the entity, or {@code null} if both are {@code null}.\n     */\n    @Nullable\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> T resolve(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        return resolve(local, remote, ResolutionPolicy.LAST_WRITE_WINS, null);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves an entity pair using an explicit policy. A custom strategy may be\n     * supplied for {@link ResolutionPolicy#CUSTOM}.\n     *\n     * @param local            Local copy.\n     * @param remote           Remote copy.\n     * @param policy           Resolution policy.\n     * @param customStrategy   Strategy implementation for custom policy (may be {@code null} if not used).\n     * @param <T>              Entity type.\n     * @return                 Resolved entity or {@code null}.\n     */\n    @Nullable\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> T resolve(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote,\n            @NonNull ResolutionPolicy policy,\n            @Nullable ConflictStrategy<T> customStrategy\n    ) {\n        if (local == null && remote == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n\n        if (policy == ResolutionPolicy.CUSTOM && customStrategy == null) {\n            // Provide clear developer feedback during staging.\n            IllegalArgumentException ex = new IllegalArgumentException(\"Custom strategy required for CUSTOM policy.\");\n            captureError(ex);\n            throw ex;\n        }\n\n        try {\n            switch (policy) {\n                case CLIENT_WINS:\n                    return preferClient(local, remote);\n                case SERVER_WINS:\n                    return preferServer(local, remote);\n                case LAST_WRITE_WINS:\n                    return preferLastWrite(local, remote);\n                case CUSTOM:\n                    //noinspection unchecked\n                    return customStrategy.resolve(local, remote);\n                default:\n                    // Should never happen; keep compiler happy.\n                    return preferLastWrite(local, remote);\n            }\n        } catch (Exception e) {\n            // Fail-safe: record the problem and default to SERVER_WINS to avoid data duplication.\n            captureError(e);\n            return preferServer(local, remote);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves conflicts for a collection of entity pairs. Each pair is\n     * represented by {@link ConflictPair}, which bundles the local and remote\n     * copies of the same logical record.\n     *\n     * @param pairs  Collection of pairs.\n     * @param <T>    Entity type.\n     * @return       Authoritative list preserving input order.\n     */\n    @NonNull\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> List<T> resolveBatch(\n            @NonNull Collection<ConflictPair<T>> pairs\n    ) {\n        if (pairs.isEmpty()) return Collections.emptyList();\n\n        List<T> result = new ArrayList<>(pairs.size());\n        for (ConflictPair<T> pair : pairs) {\n            T resolved = resolve(pair.getLocal(), pair.getRemote());\n            if (resolved != null) {\n                result.add(resolved);\n            }\n        }\n        return result;\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Strategy registration helpers\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    /**\n     * Registers a custom strategy for a specific entity class. customStrategyCache.put(entityClass.getCanonicalName(), strategy);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Retrieves a previously registered custom strategy.\n     *\n     * @param entityClass   Entity class.\n     * @param <T>           Entity type.\n     * @return              Strategy or {@code null} if none registered.\n     */\n    @SuppressWarnings(\"unchecked\")\n    @Nullable\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> ConflictStrategy<T> getCustomStrategy(\n            @NonNull Class<T> entityClass\n    ) {\n        Objects.requireNonNull(entityClass, \"entityClass == null\");\n        return (ConflictStrategy<T>) customStrategyCache.get(entityClass.getCanonicalName());\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Default strategy implementations\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @Nullable\n    private <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferClient(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        // If local is soft-deleted, favour remote to avoid phantom records.\n        return local != null && !local.isMarkedDeleted() ? local : remote;\n    }\n\n    @Nullable\n    private <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferServer(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        return remote != null ? remote : local;\n    }\n\n    @Nullable\n    private <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferLastWrite(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        if (local == null) return remote;\n        if (remote == null) return local;\n\n        Instant localTs  = local.getUpdatedAt();\n        Instant remoteTs = remote.getUpdatedAt();\n\n        if (remoteTs.isAfter(localTs)) {\n            return remote;\n        } else if (localTs.isAfter(remoteTs)) {\n            return local;\n        }\n\n        // Same timestamp – fall back to revision number.\n        return remote.getRevision() > local.getRevision() ? remote : local;\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Diagnostics helpers\n    // JournalEntryEntity entity) {\n        JournalEntryEntity toSave = entity;\n        if (toSave.getId() == null) {\n            // Generate local UUID if the entry is new.\n            toSave = by WorkManager (or app foreground) to push any locally pending changes.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public Completable trySyncPendingEntries() {\n        return networkMonitor.isConnectedSingle()\n                .flatMapCompletable(isOnline -> {\n                    if (!isOnline) {\n                        return Completable.complete();\n                    }\n                    return }\n                                                     // Merge server authoritative state\n                                                     journalDao.upsertAll(serverEntities);\n                                                 }))\n                                                 .subscribeOn(Schedulers.io())\n                                                 .retryWhen(buildRetryStrategy(\"syncPending\"));\n                            });\n                })\n                .subscribeOn(Schedulers.io());\n    }\n    // endregion\n\n    // region – Private helpers\n\n    /**\n     * Retrieves the full remote list (paged) and updates the local cache.\n     */\n    private Single<Boolean> pullLatestFromBackend(@NonNull String userId) {\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_01Fof626SgbYjnxHzhNptxWb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01U6yFaHGcfn24PxQV9jmcwM","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/logs/agent/solution.md","content":"# WellSphere Connect: Data Synchronization and Conflict Resolution Architecture\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThis document provides a comprehensive analysis of the data synchronization and conflict resolution architecture in WellSphere Connect, focusing specifically on journal entry synchronization. The application implements a robust **offline-first architecture** using Android's WorkManager, the Repository pattern, and a configurable conflict resolution strategy to ensure reliable data synchronization between local (Room) and remote (REST API) data sources.\n\n---\n\n## 1. High-Level Flow: End-to-End Synchronization Process\n\nThe synchronization process follows a three-phase transaction model with comprehensive error handling and conflict resolution.\n\n### 1.1 Mermaid Sequence Diagram\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant WM as WorkManager\n    participant SW as SyncWorker\n    participant SM as SessionManager\n    participant NU as NetworkUtil\n    participant SE as SyncEngine\n    participant OR as OfflineRecordRepository\n    participant MR as SyncMetadataRepository\n    participant SCR as SyncConflictResolver\n    participant JR as JournalRepository\n    participant API as Remote API\n    participant DB as Room Database\n    participant User as User (Notification)\n\n    Note over WM: Trigger: Periodic schedule, network change, or manual pull-to-refresh\n\n    WM->>SW: doWork()\n    SW->>SM: isUserAuthenticated()\n\n    alt User not authenticated\n        SM-->>SW: false\n        SW-->>WM: Result.failure()\n    else User authenticated\n        SM-->>SW: true\n        SW->>NU: isConnected()\n\n        alt No network connectivity\n            NU-->>SW: false\n            SW-->>WM: Result.retry()\n        else Network available\n            NU-->>SW: true\n\n            Note over SW: Phase 1: PUSH Local Changes\n            SW->>OR: fetchPendingRecords()\n            OR->>DB: Query pendingSync=true entries\n            DB-->>OR: List<PendingRecords>\n            OR-->>SW: List<PendingRecords>\n            SW->>SE: pushPending(records)\n            SE->>API: POST/PUT entries\n\n            alt API returns 409 Conflict\n                API-->>SE: HTTP 409\n                SE->>SCR: resolve(local, remote, policy)\n                SCR->>SCR: Apply strategy (LAST_WRITE_WINS default)\n                SCR-->>SE: Resolved entity\n                SE->>DB: Update local with resolved entity\n            else API success\n                API-->>SE: 200 OK + updated entity\n                SE->>DB: Mark pendingSync=false\n            end\n\n            Note over SW: Phase 2: PULL Remote Changes\n            SW->>MR: getLastSyncToken()\n            MR-->>SW: lastSyncToken\n            SW->>SE: pullUpdates(lastSyncToken)\n            SE->>API: GET /entries?since=lastSyncToken\n            API-->>SE: List<RemoteEntries>\n            SE->>DB: Merge/replace local cache\n\n            Note over SW: Phase 3: COMMIT Metadata\n            SW->>MR: setLastSyncTimestamp(now)\n\n            SW-->>WM: Result.success()\n        end\n    end\n\n    alt MergeConflictException thrown\n        SW->>User: notifyMergeConflict()\n        SW-->>WM: Result.success() (to prevent auto-retry)\n    else SyncException/IllegalStateException\n        SW-->>WM: Result.retry() (exponential backoff)\n    else Unknown Fatal Error\n        SW-->>WM: Result.failure()\n    end\n```\n\n### 1.2 Detailed Numbered Flow\n\n1. **Trigger Event**: WorkManager invokes `SyncWorker.doWork()` based on:\n   - Periodic schedule (configured via `schedulePeriodicSync()`)\n   - One-off request (via `enqueueOneOffSync()` - e.g., pull-to-refresh)\n   - Network state change (implicit via WorkManager constraints)\n\n2. **Pre-flight Checks**:\n   - Verify user authentication via `SessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()`\n   - Verify network connectivity via `NetworkUtil.isConnected()`\n   - Return `Result.failure()` if not authenticated\n   - Return `Result.retry()` if no network (WorkManager will reschedule)\n\n3. **Phase 1 - PUSH Local Changes** (`SyncWorker.java:152-153`):\n   - `OfflineRecordRepository.fetchPendingRecords()` retrieves all entries with `pendingSync=true`\n   - `SyncEngine.pushPending()` iterates through pending records\n   - For each record: POST (if new) or PUT (if update) to remote API\n   - On HTTP 409 Conflict: invoke `SyncConflictResolver.resolve()` with local and server versions\n   - On success: mark entry as `pendingSync=false` in local database\n\n4. **Phase 2 - PULL Remote Changes** (`SyncWorker.java:155-156`):\n   - Retrieve last sync token from `SyncMetadataRepository.getLastSyncToken()`\n   - `SyncEngine.pullUpdates()` fetches all changes since that token\n   - Merge/replace local cache with server-authoritative data\n   - Handle any additional conflicts discovered during pull\n\n5. **Phase 3 - COMMIT Metadata** (`SyncWorker.java:158-159`):\n   - Update `SyncMetadataRepository.setLastSyncTimestamp()` to current time\n   - This serves as the sync token for the next sync cycle\n\n6. **Result Handling** (`SyncWorker.java:106-140`):\n   - `Result.success()`: Both push and pull completed successfully\n   - `MergeConflictException`: Log, notify user, return `success()` (manual resolution needed)\n   - `SyncException`/`IllegalStateException`: Return `retry()` (transient errors)\n   - Unknown exceptions: Return `failure()` (fatal errors, stop retrying)\n\n---\n\n## 2. Component Responsibilities\n\n### 2.1 SyncWorker (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncWorker.java`)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Orchestrate background data synchronization as a deferrable, network-aware background task.\n\n**Key Characteristics**:\n- Extends `androidx.work.Worker` - the standard Android component for guaranteed background execution\n- **Idempotent**: Safe to run multiple times without side effects\n- **Resumable**: Can be interrupted and restarted by the OS\n- **Network-aware**: Only executes when network constraints are met\n\n**Triggering Mechanisms**:\n\n| Trigger | Method | Use Case |\n|---------|--------|----------|\n| One-off | `enqueueOneOffSync(Context)` | User pull-to-refresh, after creating new data while online |\n| Periodic | `schedulePeriodicSync(Context, long)` | Background sync every N minutes (called on login) |\n| Cancellation | `cancelAllSync(Context)` | On logout or permission revocation |\n\n**Constraints Applied** (`SyncWorker.java:218-224`):\n```java\n.setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)  // Must have network\n.setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)                 // Preserve battery\n.setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)                 // Need space for media\n```\n\n**Retry Policy** (`SyncWorker.java:171-174`):\n- Exponential backoff starting at `MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS`\n- Maximum backoff: 30 minutes (`MAX_BACKOFF_MINUTES`)\n\n---\n\n### 2.2 JournalRepository (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/JournalRepository.java`)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Abstract the data layer, providing a clean API for the ViewModel/UseCase layer while encapsulating the complexity of coordinating local and remote data sources.\n\n**Separation of Concerns**:\n\nThe repository implements the **Network Bound Resource** pattern (Google's recommended approach for Android apps):\n\n| Operation | Local (Room) | Remote (Retrofit) | Coordination |\n|-----------|--------------|-------------------|--------------|\n| Read | `journalDao.getAllForUser()` | `apiService.getAllEntries()` | Serve local first, refresh from remote if online + stale |\n| Create | `journalDao.insertOrReplace()` | `apiService.postEntry()` | Write local immediately, sync remote async |\n| Update | `journalDao.insertOrReplace()` | `apiService.putEntry()` | Write local immediately, sync remote async |\n| Delete | `journalDao.delete()` | `apiService.deleteEntry()` | Delete local, sync remote, revert on failure |\n\n**Key Methods for Sync**:\n\n1. **`getJournalEntries(userId, forceRefresh)`** (`JournalRepository.java:93-129`):\n   - Returns `LiveData<Resource<List<JournalEntryEntity>>>` for reactive UI updates\n   - Serves cached data immediately via `MediatorLiveData`\n   - Conditionally fetches from remote based on cooldown window (5 minutes) or `forceRefresh`\n\n2. **`performFullSync(userId)`** (`JournalRepository.java:210-262`):\n   - Annotated with `@WorkerThread` - designed for background execution\n   - Called by SyncWorker to execute the complete sync cycle\n   - Three-step process: push upserts, push deletes, pull updates\n\n3. **Offline Queuing** (`JournalRepository.java:282-319`):\n   - Entries created/updated offline get `pendingSync=true` flag\n   - Entries deleted offline get `flagAsDeleted=true` via `journalDao.flagAsDeleted()`\n\n---\n\n### 2.3 JournalRepositoryImpl (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java`)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: RxJava3-based implementation of JournalRepository with reactive streams, optimistic updates, and exponential backoff retries.\n\n**Key Implementation Details**:\n\n1. **Reactive Streams** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:87-99`):\n   - Uses `Flowable.merge()` to combine local DB observations with manual refresh signals\n   - `BehaviorProcessor` enables hot streams for real-time UI updates\n   - `distinctUntilChanged()` prevents duplicate emissions\n\n2. **Optimistic Updates** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:106-121`):\n   - Local write happens first via `journalDao.upsert()`\n   - Remote sync fires as fire-and-forget via `andThen(trySyncPendingEntries())`\n   - UI sees changes immediately; background sync catches up\n\n3. **Retry Strategy** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:250-263`):\n   ```java\n   // Exponential backoff: 2^retryCount * 5 seconds\n   long delay = (long) Math.pow(2, retryCount) * RETRY_BACKOFF_MILLIS;\n   ```\n   - Maximum 3 retries (`MAX_RETRY_COUNT`)\n   - Base delay: 5 seconds (`RETRY_BACKOFF_MILLIS`)\n\n4. **Tombstone Handling** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:144-152`):\n   - Failed remote deletes create tombstone entries with `pendingDeletion=true`\n   - Background sync will retry deletion on next cycle\n\n---\n\n### 2.4 SyncConflictResolver (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java`)\n\n**Purpose**: Deterministically resolve conflicts when the same record has been modified on both the local device and the remote server.\n\n**Conflict Resolution Strategies**:\n\n| Strategy | Description | When to Use |\n|----------|-------------|-------------|\n| `CLIENT_WINS` | Always prefer local copy (unless soft-deleted) | User-facing data where local intent takes precedence |\n| `SERVER_WINS` | Always prefer remote copy | Server-authoritative data (e.g., billing, admin settings) |\n| `LAST_WRITE_WINS` | Compare `updatedAt` timestamps, then `revision` as tiebreaker | **Default strategy** - fair for multi-device scenarios |\n| `CUSTOM` | Delegate to injectable `ConflictStrategy<T>` implementation | Domain-specific rules (e.g., merge journal content) |\n\n**Implementation Details**:\n\n1. **Thread Safety** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:38`):\n   - Singleton pattern with double-checked locking\n   - `ConcurrentMap` for custom strategy cache\n   - Stateless resolution methods\n\n2. **Default Resolution: Last-Write-Wins** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:230-248`):\n   ```java\n   if (remoteTs.isAfter(localTs)) {\n       return remote;\n   } else if (localTs.isAfter(remoteTs)) {\n       return local;\n   }\n   // Same timestamp – fall back to revision number\n   return remote.getRevision() > local.getRevision() ? remote : local;\n   ```\n\n3. **Fail-Safe Behavior** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:138-142`):\n   - On unexpected errors, defaults to `SERVER_WINS`\n   - Prevents data duplication while ensuring data preservation\n\n4. **Extensibility** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:182-206`):\n   - `registerCustomStrategy(Class<T>, ConflictStrategy<T>)` for domain-specific resolution\n   - Cached by entity class name for efficient bulk operations\n   - `SyncableEntity` interface defines contract: `getRevision()`, `getUpdatedAt()`, `isMarkedDeleted()`\n\n---\n\n### 2.5 Dagger/Hilt DI Modules\n\n#### DatabaseModule (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/DatabaseModule.java`)\n\n**Role in Synchronization**: Provides the local persistence layer with HIPAA-compliant encryption.\n\n| Dependency | Purpose | Sync Relevance |\n|------------|---------|----------------|\n| `AppDatabase` | Encrypted Room database | Stores all local journal entries, sync flags |\n| `byte[] databasePassphrase` | SQLCipher encryption key from Android Keystore | Protects PHI at rest |\n| `PatientDao`, `VitalDao`, etc. | Type-safe data access | DAO methods for querying pending entries |\n\n**Key Features**:\n- **SQLCipher Encryption** (`DatabaseModule.java:78-81`): All data encrypted at rest\n- **Migration Support** (`DatabaseModule.java:90-92`): Safe schema evolution\n- **Background Query Execution** (`DatabaseModule.java:89`): Single-threaded executor for migrations\n\n#### NetworkModule (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/NetworkModule.java`)\n\n**Role in Synchronization**: Provides the remote API client with offline support and security hardening.\n\n| Dependency | Purpose | Sync Relevance |\n|------------|---------|----------------|\n| `OkHttpClient` | HTTP client with caching and interceptors | Handles network requests, offline caching |\n| `Retrofit` | Type-safe REST client | API interface generation |\n| `ApiService` | Remote API endpoints | Journal CRUD operations |\n\n**Key Interceptors for Offline-First**:\n\n1. **OfflineCacheInterceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:152-179`):\n   ```java\n   // Accept stale cache up to 7 days when offline\n   \"Cache-Control\", \"public, only-if-cached, max-stale=\" + (7 * 24 * 60 * 60)\n   ```\n\n2. **NetworkCacheInterceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:184-194`):\n   ```java\n   // 1-minute freshness when online\n   \"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=60\"\n   ```\n\n3. **AuthInterceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:125-147`):\n   - Injects Bearer token for authenticated API calls\n   - Essential for sync operations requiring user identity\n\n**Security Features**:\n- **Certificate Pinning** (`NetworkModule.java:78-80`): Prevents MITM attacks\n- **Timeouts** (`NetworkModule.java:89-91`): 20-second limits prevent hanging sync operations\n\n#### RepositoryModule (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/RepositoryModule.java`)\n\n**Role in Synchronization**: Wires repository implementations with their dependencies.\n\n| Repository | Key Dependencies | Sync Role |\n|------------|------------------|-----------|\n| `OfflineSyncRepository` | `OfflineSyncLocalDataSource`, `AuthRepository`, `AppExecutors` | Manages offline sync queue |\n| `HealthRecordRepository` | `HealthRemoteDataSource`, `HealthLocalDataSource`, `OfflineSyncRepository` | Domain data with sync support |\n\n**Decoupling Benefits**:\n- Repositories receive interfaces, not concrete implementations\n- Data sources can be swapped for testing (mock API, in-memory DB)\n- Thread dispatchers injected for proper threading control\n\n---\n\n## 3. Architectural Pattern Identification\n\n### 3.1 Primary Patterns\n\n| Pattern | Component | Implementation |\n|---------|-----------|----------------|\n| **Worker Pattern** | `SyncWorker` | Android WorkManager for deferrable, guaranteed execution |\n| **Repository Pattern** | `JournalRepository`, `JournalRepositoryImpl` | Single source of truth abstracting local/remote |\n| **Strategy Pattern** | `SyncConflictResolver` + `ConflictStrategy<T>` | Pluggable conflict resolution algorithms |\n| **Singleton Pattern** | `SyncConflictResolver`, `JournalRepository` | Application-wide single instances |\n| **Dependency Injection** | `DatabaseModule`, `NetworkModule`, `RepositoryModule` | Dagger-Hilt for decoupled object graphs |\n| **Observer Pattern** | `LiveData`, `Flowable`, `BehaviorProcessor` | Reactive data streams for UI updates |\n\n### 3.2 Why These Patterns Fit Offline-First Mobile\n\n1. **Worker Pattern (WorkManager)**:\n   - **Problem**: Mobile apps are frequently killed by the OS; network is unreliable\n   - **Solution**: WorkManager guarantees task execution with system-level scheduling, automatic retry, and constraint satisfaction\n   - **Fit**: Sync operations need to complete eventually, even after app restart\n\n2. **Repository Pattern**:\n   - **Problem**: UI layer shouldn't care about data source complexity\n   - **Solution**: Repository provides a unified API; internally coordinates cache vs. network\n   - **Fit**: Offline-first requires serving cached data first, then refreshing - repository encapsulates this logic\n\n3. **Strategy Pattern (Conflict Resolver)**:\n   - **Problem**: Different data types may have different conflict semantics\n   - **Solution**: Configurable strategies (`LAST_WRITE_WINS`, `SERVER_WINS`, `CUSTOM`)\n   - **Fit**: Journal entries might need timestamp-based resolution; admin settings might need server-authoritative resolution\n\n4. **Dependency Injection**:\n   - **Problem**: Testability and flexibility\n   - **Solution**: Modules provide concrete implementations; consumers depend on interfaces\n   - **Fit**: Enables testing sync logic with mock APIs and in-memory databases\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architectural Justification & Trade-offs\n\n### 4.1 Why Not \"Fetch-on-Load\" (Simple Network-First)?\n\n| Aspect | Fetch-on-Load | Offline-First (Current) |\n|--------|---------------|-------------------------|\n| **Offline Experience** | Blank screen or error | Full functionality with cached data |\n| **Perceived Performance** | Network latency blocks UI | Instant local data, background refresh |\n| **Data Reliability** | Lost edits if network fails | All changes queued and guaranteed to sync |\n| **User Trust** | Frustrating in poor connectivity | Reliable even on airplanes, subways |\n| **Healthcare Compliance** | May fail to record critical data | Entries always persisted locally first |\n\n**For a healthcare application like WellSphere Connect, offline-first is essential** because:\n1. Users may be patients in hospitals with poor WiFi\n2. Journal entries are time-sensitive wellness data - can't afford data loss\n3. Clinicians need access to data regardless of connectivity\n\n### 4.2 Key Architectural Benefits\n\n1. **Resilience**: No single point of failure\n   - Local writes succeed even without network\n   - WorkManager guarantees eventual sync\n   - Exponential backoff handles transient failures\n\n2. **Responsiveness**:\n   - UI never waits for network\n   - Local database queries are millisecond-fast\n   - `MediatorLiveData` provides immediate cache hits\n\n3. **Testability**:\n   - DI enables mocking all external dependencies\n   - `SyncConflictResolver` has no Android dependencies (pure JVM testable)\n   - Repository pattern isolates business logic from I/O\n\n4. **Scalability**:\n   - Batched sync reduces API calls\n   - Paged fetching (`REMOTE_PAGE_SIZE = 50`) handles large datasets\n   - Cooldown window (5 minutes) prevents API hammering\n\n### 4.3 Trade-offs and Complexities\n\n| Trade-off | Description | Mitigation |\n|-----------|-------------|------------|\n| **Battery Usage** | Periodic background sync consumes battery | `setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)` constraint |\n| **Data Consistency** | Local and remote may diverge temporarily | `SyncConflictResolver` with deterministic strategies |\n| **Storage** | Full local cache requires device storage | `setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)` constraint |\n| **Complexity** | More code paths than simple fetch | Well-documented patterns, single-responsibility components |\n| **Conflict UX** | Some conflicts require manual resolution | `notifyMergeConflict()` with in-app UI |\n| **Clock Skew** | `LAST_WRITE_WINS` depends on accurate timestamps | Revision number as secondary tiebreaker |\n| **Debugging** | Async operations harder to trace | Comprehensive logging via `Timber`, `CrashReporter` |\n\n### 4.4 HIPAA Considerations\n\nThe architecture addresses healthcare compliance requirements:\n\n1. **Encryption at Rest**: SQLCipher with hardware-backed keystore (`DatabaseModule.java:56-67`)\n2. **Encryption in Transit**: Certificate pinning prevents MITM (`NetworkModule.java:78-80`)\n3. **Authentication Required**: Sync aborts if user not authenticated (`SyncWorker.java:89-92`)\n4. **Audit Trail**: Server-side revision numbers enable change tracking\n\n---\n\n## 5. Summary\n\nThe WellSphere Connect synchronization architecture represents a well-designed offline-first system that balances:\n\n- **Reliability** through WorkManager's guaranteed execution\n- **Performance** through optimistic local updates and reactive streams\n- **Correctness** through deterministic conflict resolution strategies\n- **Maintainability** through clean separation of concerns and dependency injection\n- **Compliance** through comprehensive encryption and authentication\n\nThe complexity introduced by this design is justified by the critical nature of healthcare data and the unreliable nature of mobile network connectivity. The patterns employed (Worker, Repository, Strategy) are battle-tested solutions that provide both robustness and testability.\n\n---\n\n## File References\n\n| Component | File Path | Key Lines |\n|-----------|-----------|-----------|\n| SyncWorker | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncWorker.java` | 86-160 (doWork), 168-206 (scheduling) |\n| SyncConflictResolver | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java` | 86-143 (resolve), 212-248 (strategies) |\n| JournalRepository | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/JournalRepository.java` | 93-129 (getEntries), 210-262 (performFullSync) |\n| JournalRepositoryImpl | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java` | 87-99 (reactive stream), 172-199 (trySyncPending) |\n| DatabaseModule | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/DatabaseModule.java` | 56-100 (DB setup) |\n| NetworkModule | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/NetworkModule.java` | 72-99 (OkHttp), 152-194 (cache interceptors) |\n| RepositoryModule | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/RepositoryModule.java` | 116-124 (OfflineSyncRepository) |\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":143,"cache_read_input_tokens":48671,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":143,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"3fe9cf7b-cb91-4f09-8eb8-c9c8448791f8","uuid":"c97f1480-dc99-4f9f-ab67-0cc74258a5b9"}

The application implements a robust **offline-first architecture** using Android's WorkManager, the Repository pattern, and a configurable conflict resolution strategy to ensure reliable data synchronization between local (Room) and remote (REST API) data sources.\n\n---\n\n## 1. High-Level Flow: End-to-End Synchronization Process\n\nThe synchronization process follows a three-phase transaction model with comprehensive error handling and conflict resolution.\n\n### 1.1 Mermaid Sequence Diagram\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant WM as WorkManager\n    participant SW as SyncWorker\n    participant SM as SessionManager\n    participant NU as NetworkUtil\n    participant SE as SyncEngine\n    participant OR as OfflineRecordRepository\n    participant MR as SyncMetadataRepository\n    participant SCR as SyncConflictResolver\n    participant JR as JournalRepository\n    participant API as Remote API\n    participant DB as Room Database\n    participant User as User (Notification)\n\n    Note over WM: Trigger: Periodic schedule, network change, or manual pull-to-refresh\n\n    WM->>SW: doWork()\n    SW->>SM: isUserAuthenticated()\n\n    alt User not authenticated\n        SM-->>SW: false\n        SW-->>WM: Result.failure()\n    else User authenticated\n        SM-->>SW: true\n        SW->>NU: isConnected()\n\n        alt No network connectivity\n            NU-->>SW: false\n            SW-->>WM: Result.retry()\n        else Network available\n            NU-->>SW: true\n\n            Note over SW: Phase 1: PUSH Local Changes\n            SW->>OR: fetchPendingRecords()\n            OR->>DB: Query pendingSync=true entries\n            DB-->>OR: List<PendingRecords>\n            OR-->>SW: List<PendingRecords>\n            SW->>SE: pushPending(records)\n            SE->>API: POST/PUT entries\n\n            alt API returns 409 Conflict\n                API-->>SE: HTTP 409\n                SE->>SCR: resolve(local, remote, policy)\n                SCR->>SCR: Apply strategy (LAST_WRITE_WINS default)\n                SCR-->>SE: Resolved entity\n                SE->>DB: Update local with resolved entity\n            else API success\n                API-->>SE: 200 OK + updated entity\n                SE->>DB: Mark pendingSync=false\n            end\n\n            Note over SW: Phase 2: PULL Remote Changes\n            SW->>MR: getLastSyncToken()\n            MR-->>SW: lastSyncToken\n            SW->>SE: pullUpdates(lastSyncToken)\n            SE->>API: GET /entries?since=lastSyncToken\n            API-->>SE: List<RemoteEntries>\n            SE->>DB: Merge/replace local cache\n\n            Note over SW: Phase 3: COMMIT Metadata\n            SW->>MR: setLastSyncTimestamp(now)\n\n            SW-->>WM: Result.success()\n        end\n    end\n\n    alt MergeConflictException thrown\n        SW->>User: notifyMergeConflict()\n        SW-->>WM: Result.success() (to prevent auto-retry)\n    else SyncException/IllegalStateException\n        SW-->>WM: Result.retry() (exponential backoff)\n    else Unknown Fatal Error\n        SW-->>WM: Result.failure()\n    end\n```\n\n### 1.2 Detailed Numbered Flow\n\n1. **Trigger Event**: WorkManager invokes `SyncWorker.doWork()` based on:\n   - Periodic schedule (configured via `schedulePeriodicSync()`)\n   - One-off request (via `enqueueOneOffSync()` - e.g., pull-to-refresh)\n   - Network state change (implicit via WorkManager constraints)\n\n2. **Pre-flight Checks**:\n   - Verify user authentication via `SessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()`\n   - Verify network connectivity via `NetworkUtil.isConnected()`\n   - Return `Result.failure()` if not authenticated\n   - Return `Result.retry()` if no network (WorkManager will reschedule)\n\n3. **Phase 1 - PUSH Local Changes** (`SyncWorker.java:152-153`):\n   - `OfflineRecordRepository.fetchPendingRecords()` retrieves all entries with `pendingSync=true`\n   - `SyncEngine.pushPending()` iterates through pending records\n   - For each record: POST (if new) or PUT (if update) to remote API\n   - On HTTP 409 Conflict: invoke `SyncConflictResolver.resolve()` with local and server versions\n   - On success: mark entry as `pendingSync=false` in local database\n\n4. **Phase 2 - PULL Remote Changes** (`SyncWorker.java:155-156`):\n   - Retrieve last sync token from `SyncMetadataRepository.getLastSyncToken()`\n   - `SyncEngine.pullUpdates()` fetches all changes since that token\n   - Merge/replace local cache with server-authoritative data\n   - Handle any additional conflicts discovered during pull\n\n5. **Phase 3 - COMMIT Metadata** (`SyncWorker.java:158-159`):\n   - Update `SyncMetadataRepository.setLastSyncTimestamp()` to current time\n   - This serves as the sync token for the next sync cycle\n\n6. **Result Handling** (`SyncWorker.java:106-140`):\n   - `Result.success()`: Both push and pull completed successfully\n   - `MergeConflictException`: Log, notify user, return `success()` (manual resolution needed)\n   - `SyncException`/`IllegalStateException`: Return `retry()` (transient errors)\n   - Unknown exceptions: Return `failure()` (fatal errors, stop retrying)\n\n---\n\n## 2. Component Responsibilities\n\n### 2.1 SyncWorker (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncWorker.java`)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Orchestrate background data synchronization as a deferrable, network-aware background task.\n\n**Key Characteristics**:\n- Extends `androidx.work.Worker` - the standard Android component for guaranteed background execution\n- **Idempotent**: Safe to run multiple times without side effects\n- **Resumable**: Can be interrupted and restarted by the OS\n- **Network-aware**: Only executes when network constraints are met\n\n**Triggering Mechanisms**:\n\n| Trigger | Method | Use Case |\n|---------|--------|----------|\n| One-off | `enqueueOneOffSync(Context)` | User pull-to-refresh, after creating new data while online |\n| Periodic | `schedulePeriodicSync(Context, long)` | Background sync every N minutes (called on login) |\n| Cancellation | `cancelAllSync(Context)` | On logout or permission revocation |\n\n**Constraints Applied** (`SyncWorker.java:218-224`):\n```java\n.setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)  // Must have network\n.setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)                 // Preserve battery\n.setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)                 // Need space for media\n```\n\n**Retry Policy** (`SyncWorker.java:171-174`):\n- Exponential backoff starting at `MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS`\n- Maximum backoff: 30 minutes (`MAX_BACKOFF_MINUTES`)\n\n---\n\n### 2.2 JournalRepository (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/JournalRepository.java`)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Abstract the data layer, providing a clean API for the ViewModel/UseCase layer while encapsulating the complexity of coordinating local and remote data sources.\n\n**Separation of Concerns**:\n\nThe repository implements the **Network Bound Resource** pattern (Google's recommended approach for Android apps):\n\n| Operation | Local (Room) | Remote (Retrofit) | Coordination |\n|-----------|--------------|-------------------|--------------|\n| Read | `journalDao.getAllForUser()` | `apiService.getAllEntries()` | Serve local first, refresh from remote if online + stale |\n| Create | `journalDao.insertOrReplace()` | `apiService.postEntry()` | Write local immediately, sync remote async |\n| Update | `journalDao.insertOrReplace()` | `apiService.putEntry()` | Write local immediately, sync remote async |\n| Delete | `journalDao.delete()` | `apiService.deleteEntry()` | Delete local, sync remote, revert on failure |\n\n**Key Methods for Sync**:\n\n1. **`getJournalEntries(userId, forceRefresh)`** (`JournalRepository.java:93-129`):\n   - Returns `LiveData<Resource<List<JournalEntryEntity>>>` for reactive UI updates\n   - Serves cached data immediately via `MediatorLiveData`\n   - Conditionally fetches from remote based on cooldown window (5 minutes) or `forceRefresh`\n\n2. **`performFullSync(userId)`** (`JournalRepository.java:210-262`):\n   - Annotated with `@WorkerThread` - designed for background execution\n   - Called by SyncWorker to execute the complete sync cycle\n   - Three-step process: push upserts, push deletes, pull updates\n\n3. **Offline Queuing** (`JournalRepository.java:282-319`):\n   - Entries created/updated offline get `pendingSync=true` flag\n   - Entries deleted offline get `flagAsDeleted=true` via `journalDao.flagAsDeleted()`\n\n---\n\n### 2.3 JournalRepositoryImpl (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java`)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: RxJava3-based implementation of JournalRepository with reactive streams, optimistic updates, and exponential backoff retries.\n\n**Key Implementation Details**:\n\n1. **Reactive Streams** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:87-99`):\n   - Uses `Flowable.merge()` to combine local DB observations with manual refresh signals\n   - `BehaviorProcessor` enables hot streams for real-time UI updates\n   - `distinctUntilChanged()` prevents duplicate emissions\n\n2. **Optimistic Updates** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:106-121`):\n   - Local write happens first via `journalDao.upsert()`\n   - Remote sync fires as fire-and-forget via `andThen(trySyncPendingEntries())`\n   - UI sees changes immediately; background sync catches up\n\n3. **Retry Strategy** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:250-263`):\n   ```java\n   // Exponential backoff: 2^retryCount * 5 seconds\n   long delay = (long) Math.pow(2, retryCount) * RETRY_BACKOFF_MILLIS;\n   ```\n   - Maximum 3 retries (`MAX_RETRY_COUNT`)\n   - Base delay: 5 seconds (`RETRY_BACKOFF_MILLIS`)\n\n4. **Tombstone Handling** (`JournalRepositoryImpl.java:144-152`):\n   - Failed remote deletes create tombstone entries with `pendingDeletion=true`\n   - Background sync will retry deletion on next cycle\n\n---\n\n### 2.4 SyncConflictResolver (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java`)\n\n**Purpose**: Deterministically resolve conflicts when the same record has been modified on both the local device and the remote server.\n\n**Conflict Resolution Strategies**:\n\n| Strategy | Description | When to Use |\n|----------|-------------|-------------|\n| `CLIENT_WINS` | Always prefer local copy (unless soft-deleted) | User-facing data where local intent takes precedence |\n| `SERVER_WINS` | Always prefer remote copy | Server-authoritative data (e.g., billing, admin settings) |\n| `LAST_WRITE_WINS` | Compare `updatedAt` timestamps, then `revision` as tiebreaker | **Default strategy** - fair for multi-device scenarios |\n| `CUSTOM` | Delegate to injectable `ConflictStrategy<T>` implementation | Domain-specific rules (e.g., merge journal content) |\n\n**Implementation Details**:\n\n1. **Thread Safety** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:38`):\n   - Singleton pattern with double-checked locking\n   - `ConcurrentMap` for custom strategy cache\n   - Stateless resolution methods\n\n2. **Default Resolution: Last-Write-Wins** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:230-248`):\n   ```java\n   if (remoteTs.isAfter(localTs)) {\n       return remote;\n   } else if (localTs.isAfter(remoteTs)) {\n       return local;\n   }\n   // Same timestamp – fall back to revision number\n   return remote.getRevision() > local.getRevision() ? remote : local;\n   ```\n\n3. **Fail-Safe Behavior** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:138-142`):\n   - On unexpected errors, defaults to `SERVER_WINS`\n   - Prevents data duplication while ensuring data preservation\n\n4. **Extensibility** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:182-206`):\n   - `registerCustomStrategy(Class<T>, ConflictStrategy<T>)` for domain-specific resolution\n   - Cached by entity class name for efficient bulk operations\n   - `SyncableEntity` interface defines contract: `getRevision()`, `getUpdatedAt()`, `isMarkedDeleted()`\n\n---\n\n### 2.5 Dagger/Hilt DI Modules\n\n#### DatabaseModule (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/DatabaseModule.java`)\n\n**Role in Synchronization**: Provides the local persistence layer with HIPAA-compliant encryption.\n\n| Dependency | Purpose | Sync Relevance |\n|------------|---------|----------------|\n| `AppDatabase` | Encrypted Room database | Stores all local journal entries, sync flags |\n| `byte[] databasePassphrase` | SQLCipher encryption key from Android Keystore | Protects PHI at rest |\n| `PatientDao`, `VitalDao`, etc. | Type-safe data access | DAO methods for querying pending entries |\n\n**Key Features**:\n- **SQLCipher Encryption** (`DatabaseModule.java:78-81`): All data encrypted at rest\n- **Migration Support** (`DatabaseModule.java:90-92`): Safe schema evolution\n- **Background Query Execution** (`DatabaseModule.java:89`): Single-threaded executor for migrations\n\n#### NetworkModule (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/NetworkModule.java`)\n\n**Role in Synchronization**: Provides the remote API client with offline support and security hardening.\n\n| Dependency | Purpose | Sync Relevance |\n|------------|---------|----------------|\n| `OkHttpClient` | HTTP client with caching and interceptors | Handles network requests, offline caching |\n| `Retrofit` | Type-safe REST client | API interface generation |\n| `ApiService` | Remote API endpoints | Journal CRUD operations |\n\n**Key Interceptors for Offline-First**:\n\n1. **OfflineCacheInterceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:152-179`):\n   ```java\n   // Accept stale cache up to 7 days when offline\n   \"Cache-Control\", \"public, only-if-cached, max-stale=\" + (7 * 24 * 60 * 60)\n   ```\n\n2. **NetworkCacheInterceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:184-194`):\n   ```java\n   // 1-minute freshness when online\n   \"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=60\"\n   ```\n\n3. **AuthInterceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:125-147`):\n   - Injects Bearer token for authenticated API calls\n   - Essential for sync operations requiring user identity\n\n**Security Features**:\n- **Certificate Pinning** (`NetworkModule.java:78-80`): Prevents MITM attacks\n- **Timeouts** (`NetworkModule.java:89-91`): 20-second limits prevent hanging sync operations\n\n#### RepositoryModule (`/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/RepositoryModule.java`)\n\n**Role in Synchronization**: Wires repository implementations with their dependencies.\n\n| Repository | Key Dependencies | Sync Role |\n|------------|------------------|-----------|\n| `OfflineSyncRepository` | `OfflineSyncLocalDataSource`, `AuthRepository`, `AppExecutors` | Manages offline sync queue |\n| `HealthRecordRepository` | `HealthRemoteDataSource`, `HealthLocalDataSource`, `OfflineSyncRepository` | Domain data with sync support |\n\n**Decoupling Benefits**:\n- Repositories receive interfaces, not concrete implementations\n- Data sources can be swapped for testing (mock API, in-memory DB)\n- Thread dispatchers injected for proper threading control\n\n---\n\n## 3. Architectural Pattern Identification\n\n### 3.1 Primary Patterns\n\n| Pattern | Component | Implementation |\n|---------|-----------|----------------|\n| **Worker Pattern** | `SyncWorker` | Android WorkManager for deferrable, guaranteed execution |\n| **Repository Pattern** | `JournalRepository`, `JournalRepositoryImpl` | Single source of truth abstracting local/remote |\n| **Strategy Pattern** | `SyncConflictResolver` + `ConflictStrategy<T>` | Pluggable conflict resolution algorithms |\n| **Singleton Pattern** | `SyncConflictResolver`, `JournalRepository` | Application-wide single instances |\n| **Dependency Injection** | `DatabaseModule`, `NetworkModule`, `RepositoryModule` | Dagger-Hilt for decoupled object graphs |\n| **Observer Pattern** | `LiveData`, `Flowable`, `BehaviorProcessor` | Reactive data streams for UI updates |\n\n### 3.2 Why These Patterns Fit Offline-First Mobile\n\n1. **Worker Pattern (WorkManager)**:\n   - **Problem**: Mobile apps are frequently killed by the OS; network is unreliable\n   - **Solution**: WorkManager guarantees task execution with system-level scheduling, automatic retry, and constraint satisfaction\n   - **Fit**: Sync operations need to complete eventually, even after app restart\n\n2. **Repository Pattern**:\n   - **Problem**: UI layer shouldn't care about data source complexity\n   - **Solution**: Repository provides a unified API; internally coordinates cache vs. network\n   - **Fit**: Offline-first requires serving cached data first, then refreshing - repository encapsulates this logic\n\n3. **Strategy Pattern (Conflict Resolver)**:\n   - **Problem**: Different data types may have different conflict semantics\n   - **Solution**: Configurable strategies (`LAST_WRITE_WINS`, `SERVER_WINS`, `CUSTOM`)\n   - **Fit**: Journal entries might need timestamp-based resolution; admin settings might need server-authoritative resolution\n\n4. **Dependency Injection**:\n   - **Problem**: Testability and flexibility\n   - **Solution**: Modules provide concrete implementations; consumers depend on interfaces\n   - **Fit**: Enables testing sync logic with mock APIs and in-memory databases\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architectural Justification & Trade-offs\n\n### 4.1 Why Not \"Fetch-on-Load\" (Simple Network-First)?\n\n| Aspect | Fetch-on-Load | Offline-First (Current) |\n|--------|---------------|-------------------------|\n| **Offline Experience** | Blank screen or error | Full functionality with cached data |\n| **Perceived Performance** | Network latency blocks UI | Instant local data, background refresh |\n| **Data Reliability** | Lost edits if network fails | All changes queued and guaranteed to sync |\n| **User Trust** | Frustrating in poor connectivity | Reliable even on airplanes, subways |\n| **Healthcare Compliance** | May fail to record critical data | Entries always persisted locally first |\n\n**For a healthcare application like WellSphere Connect, offline-first is essential** because:\n1. Users may be patients in hospitals with poor WiFi\n2. Journal entries are time-sensitive wellness data - can't afford data loss\n3. Clinicians need access to data regardless of connectivity\n\n### 4.2 Key Architectural Benefits\n\n1. **Resilience**: No single point of failure\n   - Local writes succeed even without network\n   - WorkManager guarantees eventual sync\n   - Exponential backoff handles transient failures\n\n2. **Responsiveness**:\n   - UI never waits for network\n   - Local database queries are millisecond-fast\n   - `MediatorLiveData` provides immediate cache hits\n\n3. **Testability**:\n   - DI enables mocking all external dependencies\n   - `SyncConflictResolver` has no Android dependencies (pure JVM testable)\n   - Repository pattern isolates business logic from I/O\n\n4. **Scalability**:\n   - Batched sync reduces API calls\n   - Paged fetching (`REMOTE_PAGE_SIZE = 50`) handles large datasets\n   - Cooldown window (5 minutes) prevents API hammering\n\n### 4.3 Trade-offs and Complexities\n\n| Trade-off | Description | Mitigation |\n|-----------|-------------|------------|\n| **Battery Usage** | Periodic background sync consumes battery | `setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)` constraint |\n| **Data Consistency** | Local and remote may diverge temporarily | `SyncConflictResolver` with deterministic strategies |\n| **Storage** | Full local cache requires device storage | `setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)` constraint |\n| **Complexity** | More code paths than simple fetch | Well-documented patterns, single-responsibility components |\n| **Conflict UX** | Some conflicts require manual resolution | `notifyMergeConflict()` with in-app UI |\n| **Clock Skew** | `LAST_WRITE_WINS` depends on accurate timestamps | Revision number as secondary tiebreaker |\n| **Debugging** | Async operations harder to trace | Comprehensive logging via `Timber`, `CrashReporter` |\n\n### 4.4 HIPAA Considerations\n\nThe architecture addresses healthcare compliance requirements:\n\n1. **Encryption at Rest**: SQLCipher with hardware-backed keystore (`DatabaseModule.java:56-67`)\n2. **Encryption in Transit**: Certificate pinning prevents MITM (`NetworkModule.java:78-80`)\n3. **Authentication Required**: Sync aborts if user not authenticated (`SyncWorker.java:89-92`)\n4. **Audit Trail**: Server-side revision numbers enable change tracking\n\n---\n\n## 5. Summary\n\nThe WellSphere Connect synchronization architecture represents a well-designed offline-first system that balances:\n\n- **Reliability** through WorkManager's guaranteed execution\n- **Performance** through optimistic local updates and reactive streams\n- **Correctness** through deterministic conflict resolution strategies\n- **Maintainability** through clean separation of concerns and dependency injection\n- **Compliance** through comprehensive encryption and authentication\n\nThe complexity introduced by this design is justified by the critical nature of healthcare data and the unreliable nature of mobile network connectivity. The patterns employed (Worker, Repository, Strategy) are battle-tested solutions that provide both robustness and testability.\n\n---\n\n## File References\n\n| Component | File Path | Key Lines |\n|-----------|-----------|-----------|\n| SyncWorker | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncWorker.java` | 86-160 (doWork), 168-206 (scheduling) |\n| SyncConflictResolver | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java` | 86-143 (resolve), 212-248 (strategies) |\n| JournalRepository | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/JournalRepository.java` | 93-129 (getEntries), 210-262 (performFullSync) |\n| JournalRepositoryImpl | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java` | 87-99 (reactive stream), 172-199 (trySyncPending) |\n| DatabaseModule | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/DatabaseModule.java` | 56-100 (DB setup) |\n| NetworkModule | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/NetworkModule.java` | 72-99 (OkHttp), 152-194 (cache interceptors) |\n| RepositoryModule | `/app/project/app/src/main/java/com/wellsphere/connect/core/di/RepositoryModule.java` | 116-124 (OfflineSyncRepository) |\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
